the next 8 hours · page made Sat 24 Oct, 19:24 BST · numbers from reports built Sat 24 Oct, 19:14 BST

How to read Fair and Need Fair and Need are what the evidence implies, not what anyone is offering. For a football team leg that evidence includes team strength (Elo as of kick-off, shown under the selection) as well as the record; a player leg's record is pulled towards a coin flip by as much as past picks show it should be. Need is the price the evidence supports once the sample size is paid for, and it is the one to bet off. Neither includes the bookmaker's margin, which runs 5–8% a market and compounds with every leg you add.

How the game will go

Most likely: RC Lens 2-1 Paris FC (home 52%, draw 22%, away 26%)

RC Lens 3-4-2-1 (predicted) · double pivot: Yassine Titraoui, Andrija Bulatović · behind the striker: Florian Thauvin, Florian Sotoca
They attack wide and cross (6.8 crosses a game).

Paris FC 4-2-3-1 (predicted) · double pivot: Maxime López, Pierre Lees Melou · behind the striker: Ilan Kebbal, Pablo Pagis, Luca Koleosho
They build short (only 19 long balls a game).

Why

  1. RC Lens play a double pivot (Yassine Titraoui and Andrija Bulatović) against Paris FC's 3 attacking midfielders (Ilan Kebbal, Pablo Pagis, Luca Koleosho). So the pivot is outnumbered between the lines and has more ground to cover: expect tackles and fouls from them (Yassine Titraoui: 2+ tackles 64%, 1+ foul 65%; Andrija Bulatović: 2+ tackles 61%, 1+ foul 75%).
  2. RC Lens are favourites (52% to win). So the likeliest script is RC Lens ahead and Paris FC chasing: Paris FC open up late, which adds shots at both ends and fouls from Paris FC.
  3. RC Lens are without Abdallah Sima (7 starts in 10), a regular starter. So RC Lens' attack is weaker than its last 10 say: shade their shots and goals down.
  4. RC Lens are without Ismaelo Ganiou (7 starts in 10), a regular starter. So RC Lens' defence is weaker than its last 10 say: shade Paris FC's shots and goals up.

They foul a lot (13 a game).

Why

  1. Atlético Madrid play a double pivot (Pablo Barrios and Morten Hjulmand) against Deportivo de A Coruña's 3 attacking midfielders (Luismi Cruz, Mario Soriano, Yeremay Hernández). So the pivot is outnumbered between the lines and has more ground to cover: expect tackles and fouls from them (Pablo Barrios: 2+ tackles 57%, 1+ foul 56%; Morten Hjulmand: 2+ tackles 55%, 1+ foul 70%).
  2. Deportivo de A Coruña play a double pivot (Lorenzo Amatucci and Diego Villares) against Atlético Madrid's 3 attacking midfielders (Kang-in Lee, Alex Baena, Ademola Lookman). So the pivot is outnumbered between the lines and has more ground to cover: expect tackles and fouls from them (Lorenzo Amatucci: 2+ tackles 65%, 1+ foul 78%; Diego Villares: 2+ tackles 53%, 1+ foul 65%).
  3. Atlético Madrid are favourites (62% to win). So the likeliest script is Atlético Madrid ahead and Deportivo de A Coruña chasing: Deportivo de A Coruña open up late, which adds shots at both ends and fouls from Deportivo de A Coruña.
  4. Atlético Madrid should have 61% of the ball. So Deportivo de A Coruña's defenders and midfielders do the tackling and clearing, and Atlético Madrid's wide players win the corners.
  5. Atlético Madrid run with the ball (9 dribbles a game) and Deportivo de A Coruña foul (13 a game). So expect Atlético Madrid's ball-carriers to be fouled and Deportivo de A Coruña to pick up cards.

They attack wide and cross (6.5 crosses a game); run with the ball (8 dribbles a game).

Why

  1. Internacional play a double pivot (Ronaldo and Thiago Maia) against Botafogo's 3 attacking midfielders (Hakim Ziyech, Álvaro Montoro, Matheus Martins). So the pivot is outnumbered between the lines and has more ground to cover: expect tackles and fouls from them (Ronaldo: 2+ tackles 62%, 1+ foul 80%; Thiago Maia: 2+ tackles 54%, 1+ foul 68%).

  3. Botafogo run with the ball (8 dribbles a game) and Internacional foul (16 a game). So expect Botafogo's ball-carriers to be fouled and Internacional to pick up cards.
  4. Internacional are without Vitinho (8 starts in 10), a regular starter. So Internacional's attack is weaker than its last 10 say: shade their shots and goals down.
  5. Internacional are without Matheus Cunha (7 starts in 10), Gabriel Mercado (6 starts in 10), regular starters. So Internacional's defence is weaker than its last 10 say: shade Botafogo's shots and goals up.

How the game will go

Most likely: Chapecoense 1-2 Fluminense (home 24%, draw 23%, away 53%)

Chapecoense 4-2-3-1 (predicted) · double pivot: Camilo, Yago Felipe · behind the striker: Maurício Garcez, Giovanni Augusto, Marcinho

Fluminense 4-2-3-1 (predicted) · double pivot: Nonato, Hércules · behind the striker: Agustín Canobbio, Ganso, Kevin Serna
They press hard (10.4 opposition passes per defensive action); keep the ball (56%); build short (only 18 long balls a game); run with the ball (8 dribbles a game); foul a lot (14 a game).

Why

  1. Chapecoense play a double pivot (Camilo and Yago Felipe) against Fluminense's 3 attacking midfielders (Agustín Canobbio, Ganso, Kevin Serna). So the pivot is outnumbered between the lines and has more ground to cover: expect tackles and fouls from them (Camilo: 2+ tackles 48%, 1+ foul 78%; Yago Felipe: 2+ tackles 60%, 1+ foul 82%).
  2. Fluminense play a double pivot (Nonato and Hércules) against Chapecoense's 3 attacking midfielders (Maurício Garcez, Giovanni Augusto, Marcinho). So the pivot is outnumbered between the lines and has more ground to cover: expect tackles and fouls from them (Nonato: 2+ tackles 49%, 1+ foul 75%; Hércules: 2+ tackles 41%, 1+ foul 70%).
  3. Fluminense are favourites (53% to win). So the likeliest script is Fluminense ahead and Chapecoense chasing: Chapecoense open up late, which adds shots at both ends and fouls from Chapecoense.
  4. Fluminense should have 58% of the ball. So Chapecoense's defenders and midfielders do the tackling and clearing, and Fluminense's wide players win the corners.
  5. Fluminense run with the ball (8 dribbles a game) and Chapecoense foul (15 a game). So expect Fluminense's ball-carriers to be fouled and Chapecoense to pick up cards.
  6. Fluminense keep the ball (56%) and Chapecoense sit off (14.3 passes per defensive action). So Fluminense will have long spells of possession in front of a set block: more corners and blocked shots than clear chances, and tackles and clearances for Chapecoense's back line.
  7. Chapecoense are without Kauan (9 starts in 10), a regular starter. So Chapecoense's defence is weaker than its last 10 say: shade Fluminense's shots and goals up.

How the game will go

Bahia 4-3-3 (predicted) · single pivot: Nicolás Acevedo
They press hard (10.8 opposition passes per defensive action); attack wide and cross (5.7 crosses a game); run with the ball (10 dribbles a game).

Why

  1. Bahia have a single holding midfielder (Nicolás Acevedo) facing 3 Santos players between the lines. So Nicolás Acevedo is the one exposed: 2+ tackles 63%, 1+ foul 79%.
  2. Bahia press hard (10.8 passes allowed per defensive action) and Santos like to build short (21 long balls a game). So Santos' back line (Rodinei, João Alencar, Luan Peres, Álvaro Barreal) will be under pressure on the ball: more turnovers high up and more shots for Bahia (14.3 a game usually).

Why multiples are worse than their legs A multiple is worse than its legs. The margin compounds, the legs in one round are correlated, and the shop sells accumulators because they are the most profitable thing on the counter. The same-game prices above already carry that discount; the cross-game ones do not, and are therefore optimistic.
